## 🎬 Lecture 001 — Page Speed Testing (The Right Way for Accurate Results)

**Which tools to trust:**
- Tools like **Pingdom Tools and GTmetrix "continue to provide inaccurate results and recommendations"** (per Arun).
- **Google PageSpeed Insights** uses **Lighthouse** to measure the Core Web Vitals discussed earlier.
- The only other tool Arun recommends: **WebPageTest.org.**

### ✅ Two things to do BEFORE running any test
1. **Purge all caches** — from your WordPress cache/optimization plugins (**LiteSpeed Cache, WP Rocket, W3 Total Cache, Autoptimize**) **and the Cloudflare cache too.** Needed **whenever you've changed plugins, themes or content.** (You can skip it only if you haven't changed anything in the past 24 hours.)
2. **Always test from an incognito window** (all browsers support it): **Ctrl+Shift+N (Windows) / Cmd+Shift+N (Mac).**

### 🔹 PageSpeed Insights
Straightforward: enter your URL → **Analyze.**

### 🔹 WebPageTest.org — advanced testing
- **Test location:** choose locations across continents → see how fast your site loads for visitors **near your server vs. far away**; pick from the map if you don't know which country is where.
- **Browser:** simulate loading in various browsers/devices.
- **Connection speed:** plays a major role in the **mobile score** of PageSpeed Insights. *If you wonder why your mobile score is always lower, choose **3G Fast** here and you'll see.*
- **Resolution:** browser dimensions.

**Arun's recommended settings:**
| Test | Settings |
|---|---|
| **Desktop** (most desktop users have fast connections) | Some location + a **desktop browser** · **Cable** connection · **Number of tests: 3** · **First view & repeat view** · **Capture video** · **Label: "Desktop cable"** (helps you find results in test history; **sign up for a free WebPageTest account to enable test history**) |
| **Mobile** | **4G or 3G Fast** connection · some mobile device and browser · other settings the same |

**Simple testing (if you don't want advanced settings):** enter URL → under test configuration choose **Mobile Fast 3G / 4G or Desktop** → tick **"Include repeat view"** → **Start Test.**
The **Web Vitals** section = the same data as PageSpeed Insights but with **more pictorial explanations of LCP and CLS.**

🔗 **Resources:** PageSpeed Insights → https://pagespeed.web.dev/ · WebPageTest → https://www.webpagetest.org/

---

## 🎬 Lecture 002 — Backup & Restore Your Site (Before Making Speed Changes)

There's a **small chance you'll break your site** while optimizing or configuring plugins in this section — so **have a backup in hand** to restore the last working state. Many of Arun's clients/students have **locked themselves out of the WordPress admin**, so learning to back up and restore the **database** is highly essential.

### What a WordPress site really is
Not just the dashboard, nor just the root directory **`public_html`** (which holds the files: **plugins, themes, uploads like images, auto-generated content like cache files**). **The real content of your site sits in the database.** When you mess up a plugin/theme configuration, **the files usually aren't affected — the database tables are.** So **restoring a previous working database version restores your site.**

### 🛠️ Backup with UpdraftPlus (when you can still log in)
1. **Install and activate UpdraftPlus.**
2. **Settings → UpdraftPlus Backups → "Backup Now."**
3. **Tick only "database" and untick the others** for a database backup — it's quick and light (an **entire-site backup takes longer, more storage, more server resources**).
4. Click **Backup Now.**
5. Under **Existing backups** you'll see the new backup; **clicking "Restore" on any backup restores your site to that point.**
6. Backups are stored **on your server** by default. To store elsewhere: **Settings tab → remote storage options (Google Drive, Microsoft OneDrive…).**

### 🚑 If you've broken the back end too (can't log in) — restore the database manually
1. Go to your **hosting cPanel/control panel → phpMyAdmin** (under **Databases**).
2. In the left sidebar, find your site's database. If there are many, expand one and look for a table named **`..._options`** → click it; in the right pane check the **`siteurl`** to confirm it's the right site.
3. Go to the **Import** tab → **choose the file to restore.** ⚠️ **Choose the file that has *just* the database backup, not the entire-site backup.**
4. **Don't change any other setting → click "Go."** Done — site back to normal.
5. **Didn't download the database backup?** In the hosting **File Manager**, browse to **`wp-content` → the `updraft` folder** → find your recent backup there.

### Restoring a complete (files) backup
Use an **FTP client like FileZilla**: **rename your existing `public_html` to `public_html_old`, then upload the backup and extract it.** (Arun focuses on database restore; links are provided for in-depth guides.)

🔗 **Resources:** How to restore WordPress from a backup (WPBeginner) → https://www.wpbeginner.com/beginners-guide/beginners-guide-how-to-restore-wordpress-from-backup/ · How to manually restore a WordPress website (ManageWP) → https://managewp.com/blog/manually-restore-wordpress-website · UpdraftPlus tutorial (WPBeginner) → https://www.wpbeginner.com/plugins/how-to-backup-restore-your-wordpress-site-with-updraftplus/ · UpdraftPlus backup tutorial (official blog) → https://teamupdraft.com/blog/how-to-backup-a-wordpress-site/

---

## 🎬 Lecture 003 — The Free Speed Boost: Why Cloudflare Is a Non-Negotiable

Cloudflare is "one of the biggest and best" CDNs — and **the free plan** unlocks a massive speed + security upgrade:
- A **CDN**,
- **Protection from DDoS attacks** (attempts to knock your site offline),
- **The world's fastest DNS** (helps your site start loading faster),
- **Free SSL certificates** (the **HTTPS padlock** — not just security: **a confirmed Google ranking factor**).

**Setup:** add your site to Cloudflare — sign up, add your domain, then **change your nameservers** (🧠 *like changing the forwarding address for your mail: you tell the internet to route all your site traffic through Cloudflare's network first*). **Pause the lecture, follow Cloudflare's official guides**, and come back when you get the confirmation email and the site is active.

🔗 **Resources:** Set up Cloudflare → https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/get-started/setup/ · Optimize site speed → https://developers.cloudflare.com/learning-paths/optimize-site-speed/ · Secure your website/app → https://developers.cloudflare.com/learning-paths/application-security/

---

## 🎬 Lecture 004 — The One Cloudflare Setting That Can Break Your Site (And How to Set It Safely)

From the Cloudflare dashboard: **click your domain** → on the left menu choose **SSL/TLS** (the most critical one) → **Overview** tab.

- Ignore the blue banner about the paid **Advanced Certificate Manager.**
- **SSL/TLS encryption mode:** default may be "Automatic" — click **Configure** for more control; use the **"Custom SSL"** option, where you find the encryption modes.

> ⚠️ *"A mistake here can take your site offline."* (He repeats it three times.)

| Mode | When to choose it |
|---|---|
| **Flexible** | **If you do NOT have an SSL certificate on your hosting server yet.** Encrypts visitor ↔ Cloudflare and gives you the padlock. **"The safest starting point."** |
| **Full** | If you **do** have a working SSL certificate on your server (e.g. free **Let's Encrypt** or purchased). |
| **Full (Strict)** | **The best option and the goal** — requires a **trusted** certificate on your server (the resource article shows how to create a **free one from Cloudflare itself** — an Origin CA certificate). |
| *(Strict – SSL-Only Origin Pull)* | Mentioned, but **Full (Strict)** is the goal. |
**Do NOT choose Full or Full (Strict) unless you're 100% sure you have a working SSL certificate on your host. If unsure → stay on Flexible.**

### Then: Edge Certificates sub-tab (below Overview)
- Ignore **"Order Advanced Certificate"** / **"Upload Custom SSL Certificate"** (paid, specific needs).
- **Always Use HTTPS → keep OFF for now.** Enable only **after** everything is confirmed working, to avoid being locked into an **error loop.**
- **HTTP Strict Transport Security (HSTS)** → **do NOT enable for now.** Powerful, but for advanced users with perfected HTTPS; wrongly enabled it can make your site inaccessible.
- **Minimum TLS Version** → keep the default (**TLS 1.0**; the transcript says "TLS 0") — most compatible. Higher versions are more secure but very old browsers may not connect.
- **Opportunistic Encryption** and **TLS 1.3** (transcript: "TLS 3") → **turn both ON** — modern browsers get better performance/security without breaking older ones.
- **Automatic HTTPS Rewrites** → **turn ON** — helps fix **"mixed content"** errors.
- **"Disable Universal SSL"** (at the bottom) → **absolutely do NOT click** — it would remove your free SSL certificate and break HTTPS.

*Security foundation done. Next lecture: the revamped Speed section.*

🔗 **Resources:** Adding a free SSL/TLS certificate to your website → https://aruntastic.com/resources/library/seo/free-ssl-tls-certificate · Origin CA / origin configuration → https://developers.cloudflare.com/ssl/origin-configuration/origin-ca/ · Origin health (free plan) → https://developers.cloudflare.com/fundamentals/get-started/task-guides/origin-health/free/

---

## 🎬 Lecture 005 — Caching & Speed Settings (The Final Step to a Faster Site) — Cloudflare

With security in place, unleash Cloudflare's performance features. In your site's dashboard left menu: **Speed → Settings** ("the main hub for all of Cloudflare's speed features"). Tabs at the top:

### 1️⃣ Recommendations tab
Click **"Enable all available settings."** It turns on a handful of safe, effective optimizations at once — e.g. **HTTP/2, TLS 1.3 and 0-RTT connection resumption** (transcript garbles these as "Http two deals 1.3 and zero RTT…"). Afterwards it should say all available settings are enabled.

### 2️⃣ Content Optimization tab (fine-tuning)
| Setting | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| **Speed Brain** *(transcript: "Speed. Bring.")* | Newer, AI-based analysis of your site → **leave ON** |
| **Cloudflare Fonts** | Optimizes how Google Fonts are delivered; good for privacy and performance → **turn ON** |
| **Early Hints** | Gives browsers a head start loading your site → **turn ON** |
| **Rocket Loader** | Tries to speed up JavaScript but "has a reputation for sometimes causing issues with certain themes or plugins" → **leave OFF for now**; once everything else works, come back and test — if it works, keep it; **if it breaks something, turn it off immediately** |
| **Automatic Platform Optimization (APO) for WordPress** | Very powerful but **paid and requires a plugin** → **skip** (free-setup guide) |

### 3️⃣ Caching → Configuration (left menu: **Caching → Configuration**)
| Setting | Recommendation |
|---|---|
| **Caching level** | **Standard** — "best and safest for most websites" |
| **Browser Cache TTL** | **1 month** — tells visitors' browsers to store your site's files for a month → repeat visits lightning-fast |
| **Crawler Hints** | **ON** — helps search engines like Google crawl more efficiently |
| **Always Online** | **ON** — if your hosting server goes down, Cloudflare serves a saved version so visitors don't see an error |
| **Development Mode** | Temporarily **bypasses Cloudflare's caching**; turn it ON when making many live design/functionality changes so you see them instantly. **It automatically turns off after three hours** (transcript: "the three of us"). |
| **Purge Cache** (top of the page) | After making changes and turning off Development Mode, click **"Purge Everything"** so Cloudflare fetches the newest versions of all your files |

> 🎯 With these simple free settings you have **a global CDN and a suite of performance optimizations** making your site faster and more secure for every visitor.

---

## 🎬 Lecture 007 — Compress Images in WebP Format (WordPress Plugins)

### Lossy vs. lossless
- **Lossless:** compressing **without any loss of image quality.**
- **Lossy:** compressing with **minimal loss in perceived quality.**
- Arun **advocates lossless compression all the time.**
- For decades sites used **JPEG, PNG and GIF**; **WebP** is the next-gen format with far better compression.

### Two problems when adopting WebP
1. Automatically **convert JPEG/PNG to WebP as you upload** new images.
2. **Convert images already uploaded.**
After trying many plugins, the ones that work well across many server configurations: **"WebP Converter for Media"** and **"WebP Express."**

### ⚙️ Server requirements (for any WebP plugin)
- Minimum **PHP 7** (Arun recommends **PHP 7.4**).
- PHP extensions **Imagick or GD** enabled.
- Server supports WebP.
→ If any are missing, enable via **cPanel → PHP options** or ask hosting support.

### 🥇 Plugin 1 — WebP Converter for Media (recommended)
1. **Install and activate**, then **Settings → WebP Converter.**
2. Unsure about server compatibility? Click **"Server configuration"** (right panel) and compare with the correct configuration: **items marked red must match exactly; yellow = PHP version same or above.**
3. Settings:
   - **List of supported file extensions:** enable **jpg, jpeg, png.**
   - **List of supported directories:** enable **plugins, themes and uploads.**
   - **List of supported output formats:** enable **webp.**
   - **Conversion method:** **Imagick if available, else GD.**
   - **Image quality:** default **85%**; Arun chooses **90%**. *(PNG→WebP is lossless, but JPG/JPEG→WebP is lossy — that's why a quality value is needed. **100% = lossless**.)*
   - **Image loading mode:** **"via .htaccess"** (recommended). If unsupported, temporarily choose **"pass thru"** but ask hosting support and move to the recommended method soon.
   - **Extra features:** enable **everything except "Keep images metadata stored in EXIF or XMP formats"** (rarely needed on websites). Key options explained:
     - *Automatic removal of WebP files larger than original* — deletes the converted file if larger than the source (can happen with tiny 10–20 px images).
     - *Browser caching of WebP files* — asks visitors' browsers to cache images → faster repeat visits.
     - *Cron to convert images outside of media library* — converts images pasted into the editor without going through the media library.
     - *Cron to convert images uploaded to media library* — auto-converts uploads.
     - *Force redirections to WebP for all domains* — also converts images included from external domains.
     - *Logging of errors to debug.log* — optional.
4. **Save changes.** No warnings = working. For errors/warnings, check the plugin's FAQ page (link in resources).
5. Scroll to **"Regenerate images" → "Regenerate all"** (takes time). Wait for the completion message.
6. **Clear all caches on all cache plugins + purge Cloudflare cache.**
7. **Test in a new incognito window** (non-cached view won't work): right-click any image → open in a new tab → **the image URL should end with `.webp`** → the plugin works.

### 🥈 Plugin 2 — WebP Express (next best completely free plugin)
1. **Operation mode:** **"Varied image responses"** → click **"Save settings and force new .htaccess rules."** No warnings expected.
   - If warning **"your server does not support headers in .htaccess"** → choose **"CDN friendly"** and again **"Save settings and force new .htaccess rules."**
2. Enable: **Prevent using WebPs larger than original · Create WebP files upon request · Deactivate unavailable conversion methods · Enable convert on upload · Enable alter HTML** → **Save changes.**
3. If you chose "varied responses," also enable **Direct redirection to existing images** and **Redirection to converter.**
4. Verify as before.

> 💡 **If your server is LiteSpeed:** deactivate both plugins and use **the built-in WebP converter in the LiteSpeed Cache plugin** (next lecture).

---

## 🎬 Lecture 008 — LiteSpeed Cache Plugin (All-in-One Free Speed for LiteSpeed Servers)

### What it is
- **LiteSpeed Web Server** = proprietary web server software; a **drop-in Apache replacement** with high performance/scalability — replace Apache without changing configuration or OS.
- **LSCache (LiteSpeed Cache)** = built-in, high-performance **dynamic content acceleration** of LiteSpeed server products — accelerates dynamic content, not just PHP pages; customizable; native to the server → greatly reduces page load time and server load.
- **Who should use it:** small to large WordPress sites wanting **all-in-one**: page cache, JS/CSS optimization, WebP image compression.
- **Requirements:** a **LiteSpeed web server** (to use all functions). The plugin is **free**. It includes **QUIC.cloud** (a free CDN service that **may become paid in the future**).

### ⚖️ Comparisons
- **WP Rocket vs. LiteSpeed Cache:** *if you value your time and page load time → WP Rocket is better; if you value both PageSpeed score and page load time → LiteSpeed Cache is better.*
- **Cloudways vs. LiteSpeed:** Cloudways (as of the lecture) **doesn't use LiteSpeed**, so you can't use LSCache there; they offer their own plugin **Breeze**. **LSCache beats Breeze in PageSpeed score and load time** — but **Cloudways servers + Breeze/W3 Total Cache/WP Rocket are much faster than any shared hosting at the same price.**

### 🛠️ Configuration (after installing + activating on a LiteSpeed server → LiteSpeed Cache settings dashboard)
**General**
- Choose automatic plugin updates or manual.
- **QUIC.cloud** (CDN + image optimization + critical CSS generation + low-quality image placeholders): sign up for a free account regardless; click **"Request Domain Key"** the first time (or link an existing account).
- **Turn ON "Guest Mode" and "Guest Optimization."** → Save.

**Cache** (tab) — enable **Enable Cache, Cache REST API, Cache favicon.ico, Cache PHP resources**; disable the rest. **Enable "Cache Mobile Users"** only if: your site has mobile-specific content (widgets only on mobile/desktop), you use **AMP**, you use QUIC.cloud's **Critical CSS or Unique CSS** service, or you have **Guest Mode + Guest Optimization** enabled. → Save. Leave **TTL, Purge, Exclude** defaults (use *Exclude* for URLs/categories you don't want cached; "Learn more" links give docs).

**ESI:** **disable ESI, cache admin bar, cache comment form** — *enable ESI if you use WooCommerce or have many logged-in users.*

**Object cache:** if your LiteSpeed server supports **Memcached and Redis**, status shows enabled. If not: in hosting cPanel → **Select PHP Version / PHP Extensions** → enable **Memcached and Redis**; update PHP to **7.4 or latest**. ⚠️ **Back up your site and database first.** Then enable object cache; **Method: Redis**, **Port: 6379**; leave others default → Save.

**Browser:** turn **Browser Cache ON**; leave others default.

**CDN:** turn on **QUIC.cloud CDN**. **CDN mapping**: don't turn on unless you use a subdomain + paid CDN; if just Cloudflare or QUIC.cloud, keep it off. Because we use **Cloudflare**, you can enable the Cloudflare section: enter your **Cloudflare email, domain name and Global API key** (link provided to get it) — then manage Cloudflare under the *Manage* tab.

**Image Optimization** (settings tab): **Auto Request Cron ON · Auto Pull Cron ON · Optimize Original Images ON · Remove Original Backups OFF** (turn ON if you don't want backups of uncompressed originals) · **Optimize Losslessly ON** (OFF compresses more at the cost of quality — your choice) · **Preserve EXIF/XMP data OFF · Create WebP Versions ON · Image WebP Replacement ON · WebP For Extra srcset ON** · rest default → Save.

**Page Optimization → CSS settings:** **CSS Minify ON · CSS Combine ON** (normally off on HTTP/2, but needed to use Unique CSS/remove unused CSS) · **Generate UCSS ON** (removes unused CSS). *Then clear all caches and test in incognito — **if the site breaks, switch OFF both Generate UCSS and CSS Combine.*** · **UCSS Inline OFF** (only a workaround for PSI's "eliminate render-blocking CSS" — "won't do any real good") · **CSS Combine External and Inline OFF** · **Load CSS Asynchronously ON** · **CCSS Per URL ON** (critical CSS per URL) · **Inline CSS Async Lib OFF** · **Font Display Optimization: Swap.**
**JS settings:** **JS Minify ON · JS Combine OFF · JS Combine External and Inline OFF · Load JS Deferred: Deferred** (non-render-blocking).
**HTML settings:** **HTML Minify ON · DNS Prefetch Control ON · Remove Query Strings ON** · all the rest OFF.
**Media settings** (must be right for WebP creation to work): **Lazy Load Images ON · Responsive Placeholder OFF · LQIP Cloud Generator ON** (low-quality image placeholders until images load — or turn this OFF and use Responsive Placeholder instead; your option) · **Generate LQIP In Background ON · Lazy Load Iframes ON · Add Missing Sizes ON · Inline Lazy Load Images Library OFF** → Save. Leave remaining Page-Optimization settings default.
**Tuning:** exclude specific CSS/JS from minification/combining/UCSS if needed.

**Database:** click **"Clean All"** — removes post revisions, drafts, trashed posts, spam comments etc. **Except "All Transients" — leave that as is.**

**Toolbox → Heartbeat:** turn ON **Frontend Heartbeat Control, Backend Heartbeat Control, Editor Heartbeat** to reduce server load. ⚠️ Careful — messing with these can stop WP AJAX-triggered tasks; if problems appear, **turn all three OFF.** Leave all other settings default.

**Finish:** click the LiteSpeed icon in the admin bar → **Purge All**; check the site in an incognito window; then run PageSpeed Insights. *LiteSpeed Cache performs well in both PSI and WebPageTest.* 🔑 **If you're on a LiteSpeed server, try LiteSpeed Cache first before any other caching plugin — including WP Rocket.**

🔗 **Resources:** LSCache in-depth guide → https://docs.litespeedtech.com/lscache/lscwp/dashboard/ · Plugin benchmark → https://lscache.io/benchmarks/wordpress-benchmark/

## 🎬 Lecture 009 — WP Rocket Cache Plugin (Premium All-in-One Speed Solution)

**What an all-in-one plugin should do:** lazy-load images · minify JS & CSS · generate **critical CSS for every page** · defer non-critical JS · remove unused CSS · create **page cache and preload it automatically** · integrate seamlessly with a CDN · optimize database tables. **WP Rocket is Arun's foremost choice — "better than any other premium plugin."** Super easy to install/configure, **compatible with e-commerce sites**, works with most hosts.

**Who:** simple blog to online store, small or large site — easy for non-technical users, extensible for developers.
**Requirements:** any server (**Windows, Nginx, LiteSpeed or Apache with `mod_rewrite`, `mod_expires` and `mod_deflate` headers enabled**); **PHP 7 minimum**; **pretty permalinks** in WordPress.
**Price (as stated in the lecture):** **$49 for 1 site, $249 for infinite sites**; all plans include **1 year of support and updates + a 14-day 100% money-back guarantee.**

### 🛠️ Configuration (after purchase → install → activate → Settings → WP Rocket)
**Dashboard** shows: **Clear Cache, Preload Cache, Purge OPcache, Regenerate Critical CSS, Clear Used CSS cache** — you'll use these after installing plugins, changing themes, or changing other plugins.

**Cache tab**
- **Enable caching for mobile devices:** most sites use responsive themes so no separate mobile cache is needed — enable only if you use a **separate mobile theme.**
- **User cache:** leave disabled unless you want logged-in users to see the cached version (you can always use an incognito window to check the cached version).
- **Cache lifespan:** default **7 days** — suits most sites. (Use the *"Need Help?"* buttons or the resource links for more.)

**File Optimization** (WP Rocket's speciality — "does it flawlessly most of the time")
- **CSS files:** enable **Minify CSS files**; leave **Combine CSS files disabled** *(assuming your server uses **HTTP/2**; if it's HTTP/1 and you have Cloudflare + SSL, enabling HTTP/2 is easy; otherwise ask hosting support — "highly necessary for PageSpeed")*.
- **JavaScript files:** enable **Minify JavaScript files**; leave **Combine JavaScript files disabled.**
- **Save** → check the site in incognito. These settings **won't break your site 99% of the time.** Then do a pagespeed + load-time test.

**Advanced optimization** (may break sites that aren't pagespeed-optimized — **enable one at a time and check the site in incognito after each**):
- CSS: enable **Optimize CSS delivery** and **Remove unused CSS.**
- JavaScript: enable **Load JavaScript deferred.** *Better to leave **"Delay JavaScript execution"** disabled — it may affect interactivity (each site differs; use with caution).*

**Media tab**
- **LazyLoad:** enable for **images, iframes and videos**; also **"Replace YouTube iframe with preview image"** if your theme supports it.
- **Image dimensions:** enable **"Add missing image dimensions."** → Save.

**Preload tab**
- **Preload Cache:** activate **preloading** and **sitemap-based cache preloading** — pick the auto-detected sitemap (e.g. **Yoast SEO sitemap**) or provide your own.
- **Preload Links:** enable — starts loading a link **in the background when the visitor hovers** over it, so the site appears to load faster.
- **Prefetch DNS Requests:** leave empty or enter domains of 3rd-party resources you use.
- **Preload Fonts:** enter the URL of a font **hosted on your site or your own CDN** — **don't enter Google Fonts or fonts on external domains.** → Save.

**More:** optimize your database under the **Database** tab; link a CDN under **CDN** (if subscribed); detailed video tutorials under **Tutorials.**

**Finish:** clear cache once; test in incognito; run PageSpeed Insights. ⚠️ *"Don't bother if you don't get a green score there. WP Rocket generally doesn't give the maximum PageSpeed Insights score, but your site will actually perform better"* — verify via **webpagetest.org**: check **repeat-view load time and time to first byte.**

🔗 **Resources:** WP Rocket features → https://wp-rocket.me/features/ · Starter guide → https://docs.wp-rocket.me/category/1581-starter-guide · Find the best settings for your site → https://docs.wp-rocket.me/article/1291-find-the-best-settings-for-your-site · My site is broken → https://docs.wp-rocket.me/article/106-my-site-is-broken

## 🎬 Lecture 011 — The 3-Plugin Combo (Free Speed Fix for Non-LiteSpeed Servers)

**Decision tree:**
- **LiteSpeed host →** LiteSpeed Cache (single free plugin).
- **Can pay →** WP Rocket + a free WebP converter plugin.
- **Neither (not LiteSpeed, no premium budget) →** this lecture: you can't do it all with one plugin — you need **four:**
  1. A **cache plugin** like **W3 Total Cache** or **Breeze**
  2. **Debloat** (JavaScript & CSS optimization)
  3. **OMGF — "Optimize My Google Fonts"**
  4. A **WebP converter** plugin (WebP Converter for Media / WebP Express — configured earlier in the section)
> 📈 With this technique Arun got a **mobile score of 99 and desktop 100** on a **WooCommerce** WordPress site — even on cheap shared hosting where server response time wasn't good.

### 🟢 Plugin A — W3 Total Cache (Performance → Setup Guide)
1. Install/activate (first install redirects to the setup guide) → **Next.**
2. **Test Page Cache** — measures time to first byte across storage engines (**Disk, Disk Enhanced, Memcached, Redis** if your host has them). **Choose the lowest time** (W3TC may recommend one — here **Disk: Enhanced**) → Next.
3. **Test Database Cache** — usually fastest with Disk, but W3TC recommends **Redis or Memcached**; otherwise **leave it disabled** (the server's DB engine may be faster than disk caching). If only Disk exists → choose **None** → Next.
4. **Test Object Cache** — also not recommended on Disk; choose lowest response time → Next.
5. **Test Browser Cache** → choose **Enabled** → Next.
6. **Enable Lazy Load images** → Next. You'll get a "setup complete" message with the total reduction in time to first byte.
7. **Dashboard → General Settings:** enable **Page Cache**, **Opcode cache (if available)**, **Database cache** (if you chose something other than Disk), **Object cache** (if other than Disk), **Browser cache** → **Save settings & purge cache.**
8. ⚠️ **Do NOT enable Minify in W3 Total Cache** — Debloat does that, and some necessary Minify features are premium-only in W3TC.

### 🟡 Plugin B — Debloat (Settings → Debloat – Optimize)
**Optimize CSS tab**
- Enable **Fix render-blocking CSS** and **Inline Optimized CSS** (these two create and inline critical CSS).
- **Disable "Inline Google Fonts CSS"** (didn't work well in Arun's testing — we use a separate plugin for fonts).
- Enable **Minify CSS.**
- If using **Elementor or WPBakery**, enable their respective option here; otherwise disable.
- **Disable "Optimize Google Fonts."**
**Optimize CSS – Remove Unused**
- Enable **Remove Unused CSS** — ⚠️ **resource-intensive, so don't enable it without a caching plugin in place** (W3TC, Breeze, etc.). Enable **"Remove CSS on all pages"**, disable all others → Save.
**Optimize JS tab**
- Enable **Defer JavaScript** and **Minify JavaScript**; again enable the Elementor/WPBakery option if you use them.
- **Delay Load JS:** enable **Delay JavaScript** with **maximum delay 1s**; disable all other options → Save.
**General Settings tab**
- Check **"Disable for admins"** so logged-in admins aren't served the cached/optimized version (hence **always test in incognito**).

### 🔵 Plugin C — OMGF (Settings → Optimize Google Fonts)
- **Optimize Fonts tab:** Optimization mode **Manual (default)**; **Font-display: swap**; in *Manage optimized fonts* **uncheck any font stylesheets you don't use**; **uncheck "Optimize fonts for editors/administrators"** → **Save & Optimize.**
- **Detection Settings tab:** **Google Fonts processing: Replace** → Save.
- **Advanced Settings tab:** **Remove settings at uninstall** → Save.

### ✅ Finish & troubleshoot
Reload → **Performance → Purge all caches** (admin bar) → test in incognito.
**If the site breaks:** *don't touch W3TC again* (it only has cache options). All CSS/JS optimization lives in **Debloat** — disable options **one at a time**, after each: **Save changes → empty all cache in Debloat → purge all caches in W3TC → check in incognito.** Disable **in this order:** ① **Remove Unused CSS** ② **Fix render-blocking CSS.** ⚠️ Unchecking these lowers your score in both PSI and WebPageTest.
**Only two reasons a site breaks with these on:** (1) the theme/plugins you use are **not pagespeed-optimized**; (2) the **server** misses some optimizations — on shared hosting you can't fix this; in Arun's experience, **switching to a faster host like Cloudways or GreenGeeks improves speed significantly.**
Plus: use **WebP Converter for Media** or **WebP Express** for image conversion.

---

## 🎬 Lecture 012 — What If I Don't Get a 100 PageSpeed Score? (Focus on Load Time)

> 🔑 **A green score (90+) in PageSpeed Insights is more than enough. What really matters is the actual page loading time.**

### 🎯 Benchmark page-load times
| Connection | First view | Repeat view |
|---|---|---|
| **Cable** | **2 s** | **1 s** |
| **4G** | **3 s** | **1.5 s** |
| **3G** | **3.5 s** | **2 s** |

### If you still can't hit a good score/benchmark — only 3 possible reasons
1. **Slow hosting** 2. **Non-pagespeed-optimized theme** 3. **Poor front-end plugins or third-party scripts**

### 🛠️ What to try
1. **Switch to a VPS or Cloud VPS such as Cloudways** — even the **$12 Linode plan on Cloudways** is much faster than many shared plans at that price.
2. **Avoid pagespeed-unoptimized drag-and-drop builders**; if you want a builder, switch to the **pagespeed-optimized Divi theme.**
3. **Premium themes often bundle plugins** (Slider Revolution, contact-form plugins…) that slow the site → prefer a **minimalistic, fast free theme without bells and whistles.**
4. **Avoid pop-up scripts/plugins** — they hurt speed *and* SEO (higher bounce rate).
5. **Understand front-end vs. back-end plugins:**
   - **Front-end** plugins load for visitors (e.g. **WPForms, OptinMonster, Slider Revolution**).
   - **Back-end** plugins load only for admins/editors (e.g. **Yoast SEO, iThemes Security, Akismet**) — they improve functionality and can even speed up the site.
   - The more front-end plugins you use, the more speed suffers.
6. **Remove unnecessary JS/CSS files causing trouble:** after optimizing, run PageSpeed Insights, **note the JS and CSS files listed under any "Opportunities,"** find the corresponding plugins or third-party scripts you added, and **remove them.**

---

## ✅ Section 16 Recap

- **Test properly:** PageSpeed Insights + WebPageTest.org only; **purge all caches (incl. Cloudflare)** first; **test in incognito**; WebPageTest settings: Desktop = cable, 3 runs, first+repeat view, video, label; Mobile = 4G / 3G Fast.
- **Back up before changing anything:** the **database** is what usually breaks; UpdraftPlus (DB only is light), remote storage options; manual restore via **phpMyAdmin → Import** (just the DB file) or the `wp-content/updraft` folder; full-site restore by renaming `public_html` → `public_html_old` and uploading the backup.
- **Cloudflare (free):** CDN + DDoS protection + fastest DNS + free SSL. SSL/TLS mode: **Flexible if no server SSL; Full / Full (Strict) only with a working/trusted cert.** Keep **Always Use HTTPS & HSTS OFF** initially; Min TLS default; **Opportunistic Encryption, TLS 1.3, Automatic HTTPS Rewrites ON**; never "Disable Universal SSL." Speed: enable recommendations, Speed Brain/Fonts/Early Hints ON, **Rocket Loader OFF**, skip APO. Caching: Standard, 1-month browser TTL, Crawler Hints & Always Online ON, Dev Mode for live edits, **Purge Everything** after changes.
- **WebP:** WebP Converter for Media (quality 90%, via .htaccess, regenerate all) or WebP Express; verify `.webp` URL in incognito; LiteSpeed has it built in.
- **Pick your stack:** LiteSpeed server → **LiteSpeed Cache** (free, full settings walkthrough); can pay → **WP Rocket** ($49/1 site; min settings: minify CSS/JS, optimize CSS delivery, remove unused CSS, defer JS, lazy load, preload cache/links); else → **W3 Total Cache/Breeze + Debloat + OMGF + WebP plugin**.
- **Don't chase 100:** 90+ green is plenty; hit load-time benchmarks (cable 2 s/1 s, 4G 3 s/1.5 s, 3G 3.5 s/2 s); fix hosting, theme, front-end plugins/third-party scripts.

**🔗 Section resources:** Section Notes → https://aruntastic.com/resources/library/seo/notes-wordpress-speed-optimization · PageSpeed Insights → https://pagespeed.web.dev/ · WebPageTest → https://www.webpagetest.org/ · Cloudflare, LSCache and WP Rocket links above.


> 🧐 **How to read the 🔄 blocks:** everything above this line is the course *as taught* (kept unchanged). The block below is an independent *objector* review (researched 2026-10-24) saying where the old way is outdated, wrong or beatable, and what a senior practitioner would do instead. Items marked *unverified* rest on secondary sources. A second pass re-checked the highest-impact claims against primary sources; its corrections are shown in the 🔬 box at the top of each affected block, and the full evidence is in the final appendix.

## 🔄 Objector's Review — Better / Updated Way (Section 16)

> 🔬 **Second-pass verification (primary-source check, 2026-10-24) — corrections to the review below:**
> - **Cloudflare:** Full / Full (strict) recommended and Flexible discouraged — **confirmed**. Default minimum TLS is **TLS 1.0** per Cloudflare docs (the claim "TLS 1.2 default" was **refuted**). Automatic SSL/TLS is marked default with gradual rollout (a blog says new zones from Sept 2024). Defaults of Rocket Loader, Always Use HTTPS, HTTP/3 and Early Hints plan availability: **not stated / unverified.**
> - **WP Rocket:** single-site price **unverified** (official page showed a multi-year promo; secondary sources say ~$59/yr); "Delay JS on by default since 3.16" comes from a vendor blog only.

**1. Lecture ref:** 001 (testing the right way)
- **Old way:** Pingdom and GTmetrix are "inaccurate"; trust only PSI and WebPageTest. Purge caches. Use incognito.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Partly outdated and over-strong.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Make field data the arbiter. PSI's CrUX section reflects real users; lab scores are a debugging tool only.
  - Call GTmetrix and Pingdom lab tools with different defaults, not "inaccurate". (The course claim is the instructor's opinion; no source found either way.)
  - Test at least 3 runs, because lab variance is large.
  - Note that PSI's "mobile is lower" is Lighthouse's simulated slow-4G plus CPU throttling, so WebPageTest connection profiles are only an approximation of it.
  - PSI on Lighthouse 13 now shows Insight audits, so old screenshots and checklists in the course will not match.
- **New tools/skills:** PSI API + CrUX; `claude-seo:seo-google`; WebPageTest.
- **Sources:** https://developer.chrome.com/blog/lighthouse-13-0; https://web.dev/articles/vitals

**2. Lecture ref:** 003-004 (Cloudflare "non-negotiable"; SSL modes)
- **Old way:** Flexible is "the safest starting point". Full/Strict only if you are 100% sure of a certificate. Use Flexible if no server SSL.
- **Verdict:** ❌ Outdated and unsafe advice.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Cloudflare's own docs "strongly recommend" Full or Full (strict). Flexible leaves Cloudflare-to-origin unencrypted, and the docs call it common only for origins that cannot do TLS.
  - Fix the origin instead: install a free Cloudflare Origin CA certificate (valid up to 15 years per Cloudflare's use-case page) or Let's Encrypt, then set Full (strict).
  - Cloudflare now defaults to Automatic SSL/TLS, which picks the most secure mode the origin supports. It never downgrades. It moves Flexible to Full/Strict slowly and rescans about monthly.
  - A zone can opt out with `ssl_automatic_mode=custom`. Zones not yet migrated show only Custom options. The course's "Custom SSL" navigation therefore matches only older zones.
  - Flexible plus an origin HTTP-to-HTTPS redirect causes redirect loops (third-party reports).
  - Keep Flexible only as a temporary stopgap. Never use it with logins or checkout.
- **New tools/skills:** Cloudflare Origin CA; Automatic SSL/TLS; Cloudflare SSL/TLS Recommender.
- **Sources:** https://developers.cloudflare.com/ssl/origin-configuration/ssl-modes/ (fetched 2026-10); https://developers.cloudflare.com/use-cases/solutions/encrypt-all-keep-site-secure/

**3. Lecture ref:** 004 (Always Use HTTPS OFF, HSTS OFF, Min TLS 1.0, "Disable Universal SSL")
- **Old way:** Keep Always Use HTTPS off "for now", Min TLS at the 1.0 default, no HSTS.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Outdated. (Specific Cloudflare dashboard defaults for 2026 were not confirmed from a primary page.)
- **Better / updated way:**
  - After moving to Full (strict), turn Always Use HTTPS on. Cloudflare's "Enforce HTTPS and encrypt all traffic" guide covers it.
  - Raise Min TLS to 1.2 (TLS 1.0/1.1 are obsolete; the 1.2 recommendation is general practice, unverified for the dashboard default).
  - Enable HSTS only after HTTPS is verified, starting with a short max-age.
  - The course's transcription note "TLS 0 = 1.0" matches the old default.
  - HTTPS as a "confirmed ranking factor" is true but a minor signal (long-standing; not re-verified in this pass).
- **New tools/skills:** Cloudflare Edge Certificates; SSL Labs test (unverified link).
- **Sources:** https://developers.cloudflare.com/use-cases/solutions/encrypt-all-keep-site-secure/

**4. Lecture ref:** 005 (Cloudflare Speed settings)
- **Old way:** Enable all recommendations; Speed Brain, Fonts and Early Hints ON; Rocket Loader OFF; APO skipped; Crawler Hints and Always Online ON.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Mixed.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Speed Brain is on all plans including free (conservative prefetch), so the course is right to leave it on.
  - Rocket Loader stays OFF. It conflicts with plugins and is reported to block bfcache via `unload`. If you also run WP Rocket, Perfmatters or FlyingPress, their own delay-JS makes Rocket Loader redundant. Do not run both.
  - Cloudflare Fonts is not deprecated (2026-06 changelog).
  - Always Online: no deprecation found. Some WordPress guides advise leaving it off for dynamic or e-commerce sites; this is a recommendation, not a platform change.
  - APO for WordPress is paid (the course says the same), but it is the strongest option for caching HTML at the edge.
  - Alternatively cache HTML with a Cache Rule plus a plugin for purge. Test that logged-in users and carts bypass the cache.
  - Mirage was reported deprecated on 2025-09-15 (third-party). Remove it from any old checklists.
  - Cloudflare Automatic Signed Exchanges was deprecated in October 2025 (third-party report).
  - The "Speed > Settings > Recommendations" tab layout is dashboard-dependent; verify in your account.
- **New tools/skills:** Cloudflare Cache Rules; Speed Brain; APO.
- **Sources:** https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-speed-brain/; https://perfmatters.io/docs/cloudflare-wordpress-settings/; https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2026-06-18-cloudflare-fonts-error-handling-security/

**5. Lecture ref:** 007 (WebP via "WebP Converter for Media" / "WebP Express"; PHP 7.4; .htaccess)
- **Old way:** Plugin converts to WebP and serves via `.htaccess` or pass-through. Min PHP 7, recommend 7.4. Lossless always.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Outdated.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - PHP 7.4 is long end-of-life. Run a supported PHP 8.x (version support list unverified here; check php.net).
  - WordPress core already generates modern-format sub-sizes. It supports AVIF since 6.5 if the host's Imagick or GD can.
  - Core also handles `srcset`, lazy loading and `fetchpriority`.
  - Prefer one pipeline: a CDN or image service (Cloudflare Polish/Images, QUIC.cloud, ShortPixel, Optimole) or the performance plugin's built-in converter, not a separate `.htaccess` rewrite plugin. Rewrite plugins do not work on Nginx and break behind some CDNs.
  - "Lossless always" costs size. Use lossy quality around 75-85 for photos and verify visually.
  - Also check that images are not served on 4000px originals, and that `sizes` is correct.
- **New tools/skills:** Performance Lab plugin (unverified specifics); Imagick/GD AVIF; ShortPixel/Optimole; `claude-seo:seo-images`.
- **Sources:** https://make.wordpress.org/core/2024/02/23/wordpress-6-5-adds-avif-support/; https://make.wordpress.org/core/2024/04/23/wordpress-6-5-performance-improvements/

**6. Lecture ref:** 008 (LiteSpeed Cache)
- **Old way:** On LiteSpeed servers use LSCache first. Combine CSS, UCSS, QUIC.cloud, Cloudflare global API key, remove query strings, heartbeat control.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Largely valid; some settings advice is dated.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - The plugin is still the right free choice on LiteSpeed or OpenLiteSpeed hosting. QUIC.cloud services are metered with free credits and may be paid (the course flags this).
  - Do not use a Global API key. Use a scoped API token with minimum permissions.
  - Do not "CSS Combine" on HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 unless UCSS needs it. The course admits this.
  - After any change, test INP and CLS, not just PSI score.
  - Guest Mode / Guest Optimization can change behaviour for first-time visitors. Retest consent banners and analytics.
  - Per-URL CCSS/UCSS costs QUIC.cloud credits.
  - The course's claim that LSCache beats WP Rocket "if you value PageSpeed score" is opinion. Rely on your own A/B in field data.
- **New tools/skills:** QUIC.cloud; scoped Cloudflare API tokens.
- **Sources:** https://docs.litespeedtech.com/lscache/lscwp/dashboard/ (course link; not re-fetched, unverified for 2026 changes)

**7. Lecture ref:** 009 (WP Rocket; "$49/1 site, $249 unlimited"; "leave Delay JS disabled")
- **Old way:** Prices are $49 and $249. Do not enable Delay JS. Load JS deferred. RUCSS and Optimize CSS Delivery.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Outdated pricing; advice partly outdated.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Third-party 2026 reviews list about $59/yr for one site. Prices differ between sources; check wp-rocket.me.
  - Delay JS is now a core WP Rocket feature that many guides treat as the main INP/LCP lever. Enable it with an exclusion list (checkout, consent tools, chat), and test on real mobile.
  - Modern alternatives to compare: FlyingPress (about $50-60/yr), Perfmatters (script manager, about $25-30/yr, not a cache), NitroPack (cloud service, traffic-priced). The cited CWV pass-rate comparisons come from vendor-linked posts, so they are weak evidence.
  - WP Rocket does not include image optimisation or a CDN itself (third-party review).
  - Pick one cache plugin plus one script manager. Do not stack.
  - The course's "don't chase green score" advice is fine; replace it with "fix field metrics".
- **New tools/skills:** FlyingPress; Perfmatters; NitroPack; WP Rocket RUCSS.
- **Sources:** https://www.pagespeedmatters.com/resources/guides/wp-rocket-review; https://www.pagespeedmatters.com/resources/guides/wp-rocket-alternatives; https://www.pagespeedmatters.com/resources/guides/best-wordpress-speed-plugins (all third party)

**8. Lecture ref:** 011 (W3 Total Cache + Debloat + OMGF + WebP plugin "99 mobile")
- **Old way:** Four-plugin free stack on any host.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Fragile and dated.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - A four-plugin stack conflicts with the course's own "one performance plugin" rule in Section 15 Lecture 016.
  - Prefer a host-level cache plus a server object cache (Redis), or WP Super Cache, plus an asset plugin.
  - Chasing a "99/100 PSI" score is a lab goal. Verify in the field.
  - Plugin availability and maintenance status (Debloat, OMGF, Breeze) was not verified; check the wordpress.org listings before recommending.
- **New tools/skills:** Server-level caching; WP 6.8 speculative loading; Cloudflare cache rules.
- **Sources:** unverified (no 2026 source retrieved for these plugins)

**9. Lecture ref:** 012 (benchmarks cable 2s, 4G 3s; PSI 90+ "is enough"; Cloudways/Linode; Divi)
- **Old way:** Page-load-time targets from the instructor. Switch to a Cloud VPS, a lighter theme.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Directionally fine; the metric is wrong.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - "Load time" is not a Google metric. Use LCP, INP, CLS at p75 in the field, plus TTFB as a diagnostic.
  - Hosting claims (Cloudways, Linode) are unverified and dated. Benchmark TTFB on your own region.
  - Core now ships speculative loading (6.8), native lazy loading and `fetchpriority`, so some plugin features are now redundant.
  - The "Divi is pagespeed-optimized" claim is unverified; test your own build.
- **New tools/skills:** CrUX; WebPageTest multi-region; TTFB monitoring.
- **Sources:** https://web.dev/articles/vitals; https://make.wordpress.org/core/2025/03/06/speculative-loading-in-6-8/

**10. Lecture ref:** 002 (backup with UpdraftPlus; manual phpMyAdmin restore)
- **Old way:** Back up the database before changes.
- **Verdict:** ✅ Still sound.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Add a staging site and a host snapshot for any plugin or CSS/JS-optimisation change. Back up files too (themes, uploads, `.htaccess`).
  - Store backups off-site. Test a restore.
  - Do not trust `.htaccess` edits without a copy.
- **New tools/skills:** Host staging / snapshots (for example Hostinger, per the hostinger skills).
- **Sources:** none needed (general practice, not source-checked).

# SECTION 17 — Google Search Console: Your SEO Command Center

**Lectures:** 001 Creating a sitemap with Yoast SEO · 002 Adding your site to Search Console (Domain vs. URL prefix) · 003 Submitting your sitemap · 005 Finding your top-ranking keywords.
*(No transcript for 004.)*

---

## 🎬 Lecture 001 — Creating a Sitemap (with Yoast SEO for WordPress)

### What a sitemap is & why
- A sitemap organizes your website's content so people can find what they're looking for; a **good sitemap includes all important pages** — **home page, sub-pages, and other pages you consider important**. It can help visitors understand how your site is organized, and also helps you **track how users use your site** (as described in the lecture).
- Generally sitemaps are **XML sitemaps**: just **links pointing to every page and blog post**, created mainly **for web crawlers like the Google spider** to find and index your site fast. You **submit them to Google Search Console.**
- Whenever you post new content/pages, **your XML sitemap updates accordingly**; Google crawls the sitemap **at set intervals**, finding new content faster.
- **Benefits beyond faster indexing:** helps Google **not miss any pages**; helps you **monitor all indexed pages**; if any sitemap link isn't accessible to Google, **you're notified in Search Console.**
- A sitemap with posts and pages in **hierarchical order and well formatted** helps both Google's spider **and** human visitors (but takes time on a large site).

### 🛠️ Create a basic XML sitemap with Yoast SEO
1. **Plugins → Add New** → search **"Yoast SEO"** → **Install and activate.**
2. Left panel **Yoast SEO → General → Features tab** → scroll down → make sure the **XML sitemaps feature is ON.**
3. To open it: click the **help/question-mark** button to expand XML sitemaps → click **"See the XML sitemap"** — your sitemap opens. It'll list multiple links (posts, pages and — depending on your Yoast configuration — **archives, categories, tags**). *"If you have posts and pages, that's basically good and enough."* When Google crawls it, it detects these links and follows them to lists of other links.
4. Back on the Features tab, **right-click "See the XML sitemap" → "Copy link address"** — **that's the URL you submit in Search Console.**
➡️ Next lecture: adding your website to Google Search Console.

🔗 **Resources:** What is an XML sitemap and why should you have one (Yoast) → https://yoast.com/what-is-an-xml-sitemap-and-why-should-you-have-one/ · Sitemap examples: the different types (Semrush) → https://www.semrush.com/blog/sitemap-examples/ · HTML sitemap benefits (Semrush) → https://www.semrush.com/blog/html-sitemap/ · 7 reasons an HTML sitemap is still a must-have (SEJ) → https://www.searchenginejournal.com/html-sitemap-importance/325405/

---

## 🎬 Lecture 002 — Adding Your Site to Google Search Console (Domain vs. URL Prefix)

In Search Console, websites are called **properties.** Two ways to add one:

| | **Domain property** | **URL-prefix property** |
|---|---|---|
| **What you enter** | Your domain name (e.g. `example.com`) | A specific URL version |
| **What's included** | **All versions by default**: `http` and `https`, naked domain, **subdomains** and **all interior paths** of all of those | **Only that exact version** of your domain |
| **Verification** | **Requires DNS verification** | Several methods |
| **Best for** | Adding **all versions** of your domain — "almost all of our students are good to go with Domain property" | Tracking **multiple segments** of a site (a sub-path or subdomain) |
**URL-prefix example:** a travel site with sub-paths could have separate properties for `https://www.example.com/france/`, `/ireland/`, `/spain/`, and `http://m.example.com/` (the mobile site).

### 🛠️ Verifying a Domain property via DNS (3 steps)
After entering the domain and clicking **Continue**, Google asks you to confirm ownership by adding a **DNS TXT record** with your domain provider (e.g. **GoDaddy, Network Solutions**) — **or in Cloudflare's DNS settings if you've already set up Cloudflare.**
1. **Get your TXT record** — Google shows a **string value**; copy it.
2. **Add the TXT record to your domain provider's (or Cloudflare's) DNS:** *Add a new TXT record*, paste the string; in the **Name** field enter **`@`** for normal domains (or the **subdomain name** for subdomains) → **Add record.**
3. **Verify:** open the unverified property in Search Console (or switch with the property selector) → click **Verify.** Successful attempts verify immediately; **DNS changes may take time — if Google can't find the record immediately, wait a day and repeat step 3.**
- ⚠️ **Don't remove the DNS record, even after verification succeeds** — to stay verified.
- **Adding more properties:** open the menu → the dropdown → **Add property** → repeat.

🔗 **Resources:** Domain property vs URL-prefix property → https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/34592?hl=en-GB · Solutions to common verification problems → https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/9008080#common_verification_problems

---

## 🎬 Lecture 003 — Submitting Your Sitemap to Google Search Console

**First check that your sitemap exists:** visit `yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml` or `yourdomain.com/sitemap_index.xml`.

🛠️ **Submit:** in Search Console open the menu → choose the property → under **Indexing** click **Sitemaps** → enter `https://yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml` → **Submit.**
- Success message: *Google will periodically process it and look for changes; you'll be notified if anything goes wrong.* ✅ **It's a one-time process — you don't need to resubmit after modifying sitemap content;** Google senses and crawls changes automatically.
- If errors (e.g. **404s** on sitemap links) are found, you're **notified in Search Console and by email** (per your notification preferences).
- The Sitemaps page lists all sitemaps with **submission date, last read/crawled date, and discovered URLs**. Clicking a sitemap shows the sitemaps inside it (e.g. a **page sitemap** and a **post sitemap**).

### 📊 Index coverage report (left sidebar "Coverage" / "see index coverage")
Shows **Errors, Valid URLs, Excluded URLs.**
- **Both error URLs and excluded URLs don't appear in Google search results.**
- **Excluded URLs** are ones **you voluntarily excluded** with `noindex` tags (e.g. a **secret contest page**, a **members-only free resource page**) or **duplicate URLs handled with canonical tags.**
- ✅ **The only thing you need to care about is the *error* URLs.**
- *Arun's example:* **16 errors** in his sitemap, but "issue count" of just one — these were **affiliate links blocked via robots.txt** that **Yoast had included in the sitemap** (he's since excluded them; Search Console takes time to update).
- *Lesson:* this is how to analyze errors shown in Search Console and then modify your site accordingly.

🔗 **Resource:** *Webmaster Tools & Search Consoles — links to all platforms (article)* → https://aruntastic.com/resources/library/seo/webmaster-tools-search-consoles

---

## 🎬 Lecture 005 — Finding Your Site's Top-Ranking Keywords (in Search Console)

🛠️ **Do this:**
1. Open Google Search Console, select your website → **Performance** section → shows **the queries people use on Google to reach your site.**
2. These are the **top keywords in that date range** — not necessarily the ones you targeted, but **the ones getting the most clicks** → they have the highest search relevancy to your pages.

### 🔍 Find keywords you already rank for on page 1
Click the **"Filter rows"** icon → **Position** → **Smaller than** → enter **10** → **Done.** These are the keywords for which your site ranks on page 1. *(Arun's note: for keywords up to his 10th position, writing an article about them can get him straight to page 1.)*

### 🔍 Which pages rank for a keyword
Click a keyword (example: **"AMD A10 release date"**) → go to the **Pages** section to see which posts rank/get the most traffic for it. You can also view **country-wise** and **device-wise** stats through the **Countries** and **Devices** tabs, filter them, and **export all data as CSV or to Google Sheets** (including **CTR and search position**).

### 🎯 Using the data (not just to feel proud)
- **Re-optimize for your real top keyword:** e.g. you optimized an article for **"AMD graphic cards"** but your actual top query is **"best budget AMD graphic card"** → **go back and add paragraphs related to the new top keyword or optimize the page for it.**
- **Cannibalization fix:** if **3 or more of your pages compete for the same keyword** → **consolidate the information into one of the competing pages and publish it; then 301-redirect the other pages to the consolidated page.** If those other pages matter for some reason and shouldn't be redirected, **add a canonical tag on them targeting the consolidated page's URL.**

🔗 **Resources (canonical tags):** Moz — canonicalization → https://moz.com/learn/seo/canonicalization · Yoast — rel canonical → https://yoast.com/rel-canonical/ · Google — consolidate duplicate URLs → https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/139066?hl=en

---

## ✅ Section 17 Recap

- **Sitemap with Yoast:** Yoast SEO → General → Features → XML sitemaps ON → "See the XML sitemap" → copy the URL (usually `/sitemap_index.xml` or `/sitemap.xml`).
- **Properties:** *Domain* (all protocols, subdomains and paths; DNS TXT verification — use `@` as Name; keep the record) vs. *URL prefix* (one exact version; good for segments).
- **Submit once:** Indexing → Sitemaps → enter URL → Submit; Google re-reads automatically and emails errors. In the coverage report only **errors** matter; **excluded** = intentional (`noindex`, canonicals).
- **Performance report:** queries → filter Position < 10 → click a query → Pages/Countries/Devices → export CSV/Sheets. Re-optimize for real top queries; consolidate ≥3 competing pages (301 or canonical).

**🔗 Section resources:** Section Notes (Google Search Console) → https://aruntastic.com/resources/library/seo/notes-google-search-console


> 🧐 **How to read the 🔄 blocks:** everything above this line is the course *as taught* (kept unchanged). The block below is an independent *objector* review (researched 2026-10-24) saying where the old way is outdated, wrong or beatable, and what a senior practitioner would do instead. Items marked *unverified* rest on secondary sources. A second pass re-checked the highest-impact claims against primary sources; its corrections are shown in the 🔬 box at the top of each affected block, and the full evidence is in the final appendix.

## 🔄 Objector's Review — Better / Updated Way (Section 17)

> 🔬 **Second-pass verification (primary-source check, 2026-10-24) — corrections to the review below:**
> - **Coverage → Page indexing:** the rename wording and menu path are not stated on Google's help page — unverified in detail. **Confirmed:** regex filter, branded-queries filter, BigQuery bulk export. **Links report as a top-level item** — unverified.
> - **Disavow:** only for manual actions / likely manual actions, domain properties unsupported, Links report row caps (~1,000 on screen / 100,000 in some exports) — **confirmed.**

**1. Lecture ref:** 001 (sitemap with Yoast: "a sitemap helps you track how users use your site")
- **Old way:** Yoast generates XML sitemaps. Copy `/sitemap_index.xml`.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Mostly correct, with one wrong claim.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - A sitemap does not track user behaviour. It lists canonical, indexable URLs for discovery.
  - Core WordPress has had its own XML sitemaps since 5.5 (long-standing; not re-sourced). Do not run two sitemap generators.
  - Include only indexable 200 URLs. The course's own example of affiliate links blocked by robots.txt shows the failure mode.
  - Keep `lastmod` accurate.
  - Reference the sitemap in `robots.txt`.
- **New tools/skills:** `claude-seo:seo-sitemap`.
- **Sources:** https://yoast.com/what-is-an-xml-sitemap-and-why-should-you-have-one/ (course link, not re-fetched)

**2. Lecture ref:** 002 (Domain vs URL-prefix)
- **Old way:** Prefer a Domain property (DNS TXT verification). URL-prefix for segments.
- **Verdict:** ✅ Still correct.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Add the Domain property for all data, and add URL-prefix properties for sub-folders or subdomains you want to isolate.
  - The disavow tool and some legacy-style tools work only on URL-prefix properties (third-party report), so create a URL-prefix property if you need disavow.
  - Bulk data export requires owner access.
  - If you moved to Cloudflare, add the TXT record there. The course already says so.
- **New tools/skills:** `claude-seo:seo-google` (GSC API).
- **Sources:** https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/34592?hl=en-GB (course link); https://almcorp.com/blog/google-disavow-tool/ (third party)

**3. Lecture ref:** 003 ("Coverage" / index coverage report; "only errors matter"; Excluded = voluntary)
- **Old way:** Read the Coverage report. Care only about errors. Excluded means intentional.
- **Verdict:** ❌ Outdated name and wrong conclusion.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - The report is now **Indexing > Pages** ("Page indexing"). Google's help page describes it as showing which pages Google tried to crawl and whether it indexed them.
  - The report labels "Why pages aren't indexed", not only errors.
  - The important not-indexed statuses are often not errors. "Crawled - currently not indexed" and "Discovered - currently not indexed" signal quality or priority problems, and resubmitting an unchanged page usually does not help.
  - Excluded is not always voluntary. It also holds duplicates, canonical choices by Google, soft 404s and more.
  - Work from the sitemap filter. Compare submitted vs indexed.
  - Use URL Inspection for one URL, and the Crawl Stats report for crawl budget and server issues.
  - Rename date for Coverage to Page indexing: not confirmed here.
- **New tools/skills:** URL Inspection API; `claude-seo:seo-google`; `claude-seo:seo-technical`.
- **Sources:** https://support.google.com/webmasters/answer/10264824?hl=en; https://rightblogger.com/blog/crawled-currently-not-indexed (third party, 2026)

**4. Lecture ref:** 003 ("submit once, no need to resubmit")
- **Old way:** Submit the sitemap once; Google rereads it.
- **Verdict:** ✅ Correct.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Also check the sitemap's "Last read", discovered URL count and status.
  - Fix the sitemap, not the report, when errors show.
  - For rapid discovery on non-Google engines use IndexNow (`claude-seo:seo-technical` covers it).
- **New tools/skills:** `claude-seo:seo-sitemap`.
- **Sources:** none beyond the course (general practice).

**5. Lecture ref:** 005 (top keywords: Performance report, Position < 10, export CSV)
- **Old way:** Filter Position < 10, find top queries and pages, export, fix cannibalization.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Works, but several newer features are missing.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - Use regex filters (custom filter on Query or Page, RE2 syntax, with a "Doesn't match regex" option). Launched April 2021 per Google's blog; this is not new but is missing from the course.
  - Use the Branded queries filter, which splits branded vs non-branded. Google expanded it to eligible sites in 2026 (March per one source, April per another). It is an AI-assisted classifier and can misclassify. It is not regex and has no ranking effect.
  - A new Insights card on branded vs non-branded clicks was reported by one source (unverified).
  - Bulk data export to BigQuery (announced Feb 2023) gives daily full performance data, except anonymized queries. Only owners can set it up. The first export can take up to 48 hours (third-party guide). Use it to get past the UI/API row limits.
  - New Generative AI performance reports (announced 2026-06-03): impressions only for AI Overviews, AI Mode and Discover, by page, country, date and device. No clicks, CTR or queries. The data is still inside the main Performance report. Do not add the two. Rollout was to a subset at first; one source says all sites by 2026-08-31 (unverified).
  - A reported September 2026 "web multimodal Search" reporting addition is on the Search Central blog archive list but its content was not read.
  - "Position < 10" as "page 1" is a rough heuristic. Average position blends many queries and devices. Use query x page grain and a longer date range.
  - Cannibalization advice (consolidate then 301, or canonical) is sound but check intent first. Different intents can legitimately rank.
- **New tools/skills:** GSC regex and branded filters; BigQuery bulk export; Search Analytics API; `claude-seo:seo-google`; `claude-seo:seo-geo` for AI visibility.
- **Sources:** https://developers.google.com/search/blog/2021/04/performance-report-data-filtering; https://developers.google.com/search/blog/2023/02/bulk-data-export; https://developers.google.com/search/blog/2026/06/gen-ai-performance-reports (Google; body not fully read); https://searchengineland.com/google-expands-search-console-branded-queries-filter-471387 (title unverified, URL as returned: https://searchengineland.com/google-search-console-branded-queries-filter-expands-471387)

**6. Lecture ref:** (not covered) Links report, Removals, Crawl Stats, disavow
- **Old way:** Section 17 does not cover them.
- **Verdict:** ⚠️ Incomplete.
- **Better / updated way:**
  - The disavow tool still exists in 2026 per third-party blogs, but Google's guidance limits it to an active manual action or a clearly imminent one. Google has not promised to keep it. A claimed March 2026 Mueller statement is unverified.
  - Use the Links report export as the starting point for a disavow file only in those cases.
  - Status of the old "legacy tools" move (Links report) was not confirmed in this research.
- **New tools/skills:** GSC Links report; `claude-seo:seo-backlinks`.
- **Sources:** https://almcorp.com/blog/google-disavow-tool/; https://linkbuildingjournal.co.uk/google-disavow-tool-2026/ (third party)
